## #225 stream OBS RECORDING upscaled 1080p→4K — record native 1080p (2026-06-24, worker, v1.7.0-dev.99)

- Validated STILL_VALID on the live stream box (10.77.9.204): `ffprobe` of the latest recording `2026-06-23 20-32-26.mp4` = **3840x2160 h264 30fps** while the OBS canvas/output is 1920x1080 (`[Video] Base/Output CX/CY=1920x1080`). #196's "stream is 1080p" was about the CANVAS; the RECORDING was the 4K-upscaled one. Bug real.
- ROOT (active `Stream_Obs` profile, `[Output] Mode=Advanced`): recording reused the streaming encoder (`[AdvOut] RecEncoder=none` → reuse-stream) and the stream encoder rescales to 4K (`Rescale=true RescaleRes=3840x2160 RescaleFilter=4`) — that 4K stream is the PROD broadcast to the local restreamer (`rtmp://127.0.0.1:1234/live`, Restreamer PID 6112). So 1080p program → NVENC-upscaled 4K → encoded into the recording. The recording's own rescale (`RecRescale=false RecRescaleRes=1920x1080`) was inactive while RecEncoder=none.
- FIX (OBS-settings change on the box, NOT repo code): `RecEncoder=none` → `RecEncoder=obs_nvenc_h264_tex` (dedicated rec encoder), `RecRescale=false` → records the native 1920x1080 canvas. Stream keys (`Encoder/Rescale/RescaleRes`) LEFT UNTOUCHED → prod stream to restreamer unchanged. Backed up basic.ini (basic.ini.bak-225-20260624-210142) first.
- Apply: `[AdvOut]/*` is NOT hot-applied (output created at OBS launch). The genlock OBS build 32.1.2 / ws 5.7.3 does NOT expose `ExitOBS` (code 204) → StopStream/StopRecord over WS (both already inactive — rig free), force-kill obs64 (PID 3588), clear `.sentinel\*`, relaunch cwd=bin\64bit with OBS_GENLOCK_WALL_CLOCK=1. Healthy: PID 17224, 1330MB, port 4455, genlock tick ENABLED + DistroAV loaded + NDI output started, no locale/video failure.
- VERIFIED: fresh 12s test recording `2026-06-24 21-04-12.mp4` = **1920x1080 h264 30fps** (was 4K). OBS encoder log: `advanced_video_recording` width 1920 height 1080 (no GPU scaling); `advanced_video_stream` width 3840 height 2160 + "GPU scaling enabled" → PROD STREAM UNAFFECTED. Test clip deleted after.
- Before→after recording: 3840x2160 → 1920x1080. Stream encoder: 3840x2160 (unchanged). Result: crisp burns, ~4x faster proof decodes (helps #226 cam1 over-count + the optical/burn reads).
- Documented the correct recording config + apply path in the obs-ops skill ("Recording Output = native 1080p") and the ExitOBS-unavailable gotcha; updated the e2e skill #225 note (now FIXED persistently). Repo change = docs/skills only (the fix itself is a persistent OBS profile setting). Version 1.7.0-dev.99.

## #128 — deterministic env-safe genlock OBS relaunch wrapper (2026-06-25)
- PR #231 (dev->main). Commits: 03b70a253 (feat: wrapper+test+skill), 8d1fadb43 (review fixes), c23e78265 (playbook PEB snippet). Closes #128.
- PROBLEM (the recurring "stale-env trap", systematized): an OBS relaunch from a long-lived win-* MCP shell whose env snapshot predates the Machine-scope write → child obs64 inherits the STALE snapshot → a genlock var UNSET → render tick silently OFF, genlock guarantee gone invisibly (#126 near-miss; #110's manual recovery was this exact issue done by hand). `$env:` read in that stale shell agrees with the wrong value — only the launched process's own PEB env is truth.
- VALIDATED still real (ticket-validator + live): NO launch wrapper existed in the repo or on either box; obs-ops skill documented the recipe in prose only, no scripted+verified mechanism. Both boxes' running OBS were already correct (PEB-read pre-check), but the deterministic relaunch mechanism was the gap.
- FIX: scripts/launch-obs-genlock.sh — pure testable PLANNER (same model as recording-verdict-on-stream.sh; scp/ssh to Windows denied, agent drives win-* MCP). Emits a self-contained PowerShell program: reads the four OBS_GENLOCK_* vars FRESH from Machine scope, sets them EXPLICIT in the spawning shell (defeats stale snapshot), clears .sentinel\*, launches obs64 cwd=bin\64bit, then VERIFIES child PEB env (NtQueryInformationProcess+ReadProcessMemory via Add-Type) matches Machine AND log shows `render tick ENABLED`, exits non-zero (fail loud) otherwise. --force=documented obs-ops kill; no-force refuses double-launch. Reserve/audit absence = non-fatal WARNING (PEB already proves RESERVE_MS=3, source-dependent).
- TESTS: tests/launch_obs_genlock.rs — 14 guards (sources the script, asserts: exactly the 4 vars; reads Machine; sets explicit BEFORE launch; cwd=bin\64bit; clears sentinels; PEB-verify+fail-loud; render-tick/reserve log checks; final verdict gates exit0 on $pebOk -and $tickOk; force vs no-force; CLI box→MCP mapping; source-safety; trailing-flag exit-2; single-quote '' escape). RED-proven (remove explicit env-set → stale-trap test fails; remove need_val → exit-2 test fails).
- PEB OFFSETS (x64): PEB+0x20=ProcessParameters, RTL+0x80=Environment, +0x3F0=EnvironmentSize. Confirmed reading correct values live on both boxes.
- LIVE PROOF (via the wrapper, both boxes 2026-06-25): strih obs64 PID 6244 + stream obs64 PID 10844 each PEB-verified WALL_CLOCK=1 RESERVE_MS=3 TS_ALIGN=1 PRELOAD_FRAMES=1 + render tick ENABLED; stream further showed timestamp-aligned release ENABLED + sub-frame jitter reserve=3ms + live reserve_ms=3 audits (had a live 2ME PGM source). Both left on #184-validated reserve=3, healthy, NOT on a wrong config.
- SIBLINGS (out of scope here): #121 post-deploy verify + #123 pre-test gate should assert render-tick (this issue is the LAUNCH wrapper + child-PEB verify, distinct from those).
- DECISION: gated the hard FAIL on the always-emitted `render tick ENABLED` line + PEB (not on the source-dependent reserve audit) — so an off-air box with a dark program scene still verifies correctly.

## #239 — windows-genlock manifest GREEN: generate completeness guard + no proc-sub truncation (2026-06-25)
- PR #241 (dev->main), Closes #239. RED 672e52273, GREEN 7b9b4fe28. Version already 1.7.0-dev.108 (>main .107) — RE-DISPATCH, no re-bump.
- VALIDATION (verify-issue-still-valid): the dispatch's premise was WRONG and corrected from primary sources. Dispatch said "generate loop stopped partway on Windows git-bash, manifest MISSING obs-plugins/64bit/*, generate exited 0 but check caught it". The FAILED main run (28156153430, sha ada26de7) log PROVES otherwise: the generated manifest CONTAINED 223 obs-plugins entries (complete), and the check flagged files STARTING AT bin/64bit/Qt6Core.dll (the FIRST bin/64bit file, which IS in the manifest right above) — a random ~half. Real cause = the CHECKER's SIGPIPE race (printf|grep-qxF per file; grep -q early-exit triggers SIGPIPE; pipefail poisoned exit status). ALREADY FIXED on dev by the prior #239 worker (RED 43ddf6c4a then GREEN cb4139542: single-pass comm over LC_ALL=C-sorted lists). The in-progress dev run 28158710742 on 731ba480 was that fix's live Windows verification (cancelled when my new HEAD dispatch superseded it).
- MY ADD (inverse guard, defense-in-depth): a genuinely TRUNCATED generate (git-bash proc-sub FIFO cut short — the failure the build superficially LOOKED like) had no guard. (1) assert_manifest_complete EXPECTED ACTUAL exits 22 (distinct from --check's 21), names both counts; (2) generate_manifest materialises stage_files into a var + iterates with here-string (no done < proc-sub), counts intended-vs-written, asserts equal. main()'s manifest=$(generate_manifest) under set -e aborts before writing on a 22.
- RED then GREEN proven at SHELL level (Tier-0 blocks the test runner): no 'manifest completeness' line + no assert_manifest_complete fn pre-fix; post-fix healthy 1501-file generate prints "1501 staged == 1501 manifested — OK" rc0, assert_manifest_complete 1501 800 gives rc22 + both counts, 1501 1501 gives rc0, --check clean x5 (SIGPIPE intact), empty stage OK. Linux PR CI runs the Rust tests.
- WINDOWS VERIFY: dispatched windows-genlock.yml on HEAD 7b9b4fe2 (run 28159329947) — the ONLY way to verify the Windows-specific fix (the broken step never ran green on a real Windows bundle before). Gated the PR on it + obs-genlock-windows-x64 artifact.
- .pdb DECISION: KEPT debug symbols in the bundle (NOT excluded). Not the bug (checker was); valuable for rig crash-debug (comprehensive-logging); prior GREEN builds shipped them; manifest handles all files incl .pdb. Excluding = out-of-scope scope-creep on an urgent fix.
- CI-GAP follow-up: filed #240 — windows-genlock manifest step must gate PRs touching genlock/manifest (full Windows build is workflow_dispatch-only + manifest logic was Linux-unit-tested only = why #120 shipped Windows-broken). Cheapest fix: synthetic real-sized stage on windows-2022, no OBS compile.
- #235 DEPLOY (separate, still PENDING): the merged #235 single-knob genlock build needs deploying to strih+stream with OBS_GENLOCK_LATENCY_MS=3 + 0-loss re-validate. Prior worker died before it. Blocked until a GREEN deployable Windows genlock build exists (this PR unblocks it). Reported, not yet done by this worker.

## 2026-06-25 — #130 + #135 cam2 display-path bug-fixes (bundle, one PR, v1.7.0-dev.110)
- BUMP 29fb398c2 (1.7.0-dev.109→110).
- VALIDATED both STILL_VALID live on cam2 (10.77.9.62) before code:
  - #130: live journal showed `34359738369 capture-dropped` (= 8*2^32+1, the k*2^32+1 underflow) at steady 30fps emitted / ~61fps captured; spurious `NDI display: No frames received for 5 seconds` + `No frames for 10 seconds, reconnecting` flooding every ~12s during normal flow. Proved old `sequence_gap(12,10)=4294967293` at rustc shell.
  - #135: cam2 DRM sysfs `card1-HDMI-A-2: status=connected enabled=enabled modes=[1920x1080,...]`, fb0=1920x1080 1:1 (no upscale → why cam2 not spiking); HDMI-A-1/-3 disconnected enabled=disabled modes=[] (the exact phantom format). cam2 is the connected-1:1 no-regression verification target.
- #130 RED a73b77e11 → GREEN 8f7031adf (Closes #130). sequence_gap: forward wrapping distance > u32::MAX/2 ⇒ backward step ⇒ 0; small forward distance (incl. legit u32 wrap MAX→0) still counts. Kept the existing sequence_gap_handles_u32_wrap + counts_skipped_frames green. ndi_display: new NoFrameLevel/no_frame_log_level — no-frame gap is DEBUG until a frame was actually delivered on the connection; only a real stall (frames flowed then stopped) stays WARN.
- #135 RED 5b72e903e → GREEN 4e6960b5f (Closes #135). clamp_render_dims = min(fb,source) per axis (never upscale beyond source; the 1080→4K nested-loop never runs). connector_is_connected (status.trim()=="connected") + any_connector_connected(/sys/class/drm) — PLAIN sysfs read, NO drm crate (probe-feature-only; --display is default-feature). Display loop polls connector ~1/sec, skips render+upscale when disconnected (phantom fb), renders on Some(false)→skip / None→render (never go dark). display_frame writes clamped render left-aligned, letterboxes the rest. incident_scenario regression test proves both guards neutralize the documented incident inputs + healthy 1:1 no-regression.

## 2026-06-27 — autopilot: #266 NDI-receive stuck-state watchdog REDESIGN (PR #270, v1.7.0-dev.120) — PUSHED, not merged
- #266 was auto-closed by PR #268's merge (its body still had `Closes #266`) despite the watchdog being SPLIT OUT for redesign → reopened; validated still real (watchdog absent on main; #265 collapse verified live).
- Built `scripts/stuck-watchdog.py` fresh (the old one generated 14+ false-neg/false-pos findings over 2 rounds). RED 3cbad2141 → GREEN a6504db34; refinements 590b35801 (sustained-freeze+staleness) + 916626d85 (deep-self-review fixes). 49 watchdog tests (92 total) green; ci.yml python job green.
- DISCRIMINATION (the core): read a WINDOW (60≈5min) of genlock-fifo audit per source. STUCK = was-HEALTHY (peak fps≥floor in window, the collapsed-vs-always-low baseline) + NOW collapsed (recent fps<floor, incl 0fps) + STARVED (recent underruns climbing + window overruns NOT dominant + recent depth≤floor). benign IDLE = never-delivered (peak~0) / steady-low / overrun-dominant. NAMED = stricter (any below-floor → alert; absent/stale → vanished, single FRESH sample NOT vanished). dantesync runaway checked INDEPENDENTLY. STALENESS (`--now`=box clock): newest sample older than max-age → skip (named→vanished); `age>max_age` not abs (future sample = freshest, never stale). Robust parse: field-by-field (#148 holds=), tail sized to window, pair_dt floor, midnight-wrap only on large-negative, per-window cadence for missing ts. Exits 0/1/2/3 (3=delivery-FAILED).
- TWO self-reviews (correctness + discrimination, parallel agents) found: lax absolute-counter was_delivering + no baseline (steady-low false-pos) → peak-fps baseline; is_stale abs() flipping future/skewed sample to stale → `age>max_age`; recent-only overrun gate → window-level; named single-sample false-vanish → absent/stale only. All fixed + locked by tests.
- LIVE-RIG VERIFY (read-only, win-strih MCP, no Discord sent): real strih OBS log (cam1=`NDI cam5` clean 30fps post-19:45-reboot, collapsed+froze 20:11 received=41421 underruns climbing ~30/s depth=0, logging stopped 20:13:56). Watchdog vs current state (now=03:59, dantesync 0%) → OK, NO false alert (frozen tail 7.7h stale/off-program); vs collapse caught live (now=20:14:30) → stuck alert; dantesync 97% no-audit → runaway alert.
- DO NOT MERGE (per dispatch) — supervisor reviews + merges. Script-only, Tier-0, no deploy.
- 📔 Playbook: genlock skill gained a "#266 stuck-state watchdog" section (audit-log location + LastWriteTime-stale gotcha, the #265 signature, the discrimination design, the --now/staleness rule, the run recipe); CLAUDE.md router updated.

## 2026-06-27 — autopilot: #147 genlock SINK ts-align backward-clock-step recovery (v1.7.0-dev.121) — PUSHED, not merged
- VALIDATED still real: read the current obs-source.c ts-align release (post-#148 `wall_now` hoist, #257 always-on) — the `due==0` branch unconditionally `genlock_holds++; return false;` with NO backward-step guard; the cam-EMIT twin guard #131 exists (`src/ndi.rs genlock_emit_gate`, test ndi.rs:1608) but the SINK had none. RED test reproduced the freeze.
- BUG: backward wall-clock step → `present_ts = wall_now - reserve` regresses below every queued (pre-step) frame ts → due==0 forever → HOLD/FREEZE of the live program feed (always-on since #257).
- FIX (mirror #131): HEAD-FUTURE detection — a queued head stamped `> wall_now + interval` is impossible for a live capture (captured before the step) → on due==0 with such a head RE-ANCHOR (present newest, drop stale). Chose head-future over a stored `last_wall_now` one-shot because the stale future-stamped head PERSISTS over the seam (one-shot would re-freeze next tick); head-future self-heals, stateless. Large per-source latency (≤2000ms) is past-stamped → never triggers.
- C: vendor obs-source.c ts-align `due==0` branch (~4832-4880) → `head_future` check + `genlock_backward_steps++` re-anchor + `to_drop = num-1`; obs-internal.h `genlock_backward_steps` field; audit line gained `backward_steps=%llu`. Rust mirror: `genlock_release_guarded`/`GenlockReleaseGuarded` (src/probe/genlock.rs).
- RED 08986c8d4 (`test:[red]` — sink_backward_clock_step_re_anchors_instead_of_freezing + sink_backward_step_recovers_within_one_interval_not_frozen_forever, verified FAIL) → GREEN aa8290d83 (`fix:[green] Closes #147`, verified PASS). Version bump f2fa7ed2c.
- Guards: tests/genlock_preload.rs vendored-source guard (re-anchor + counter + struct field + audit token) + 2 workflow-gate guards; #147 pwsh token gate added to BOTH windows-genlock.yml + windows-genlock-fast.yml (#269 lock-step). Local: --features probe → 44 lib genlock + 84 genlock_preload guards green; Tier-0 default gate (fmt/check/clippy/test --no-run) clean.
- GOTCHA hit: the existing `ts_align_hold_counts_as_hold_not_underrun` guard pins `genlock_holds++` within 2500 chars of the reserve-deadline anchor; the #147 comment block pushed it to 2714 → trimmed the due==0 comments back to 2382. Captured in the genlock skill.
- DEPLOY: this carries the prior-merged-but-not-yet-deployed #259/#200/#148 genlock-audit changes too; supervisor does the 150-min build + rig deploy-verify (render tick ENABLED, audit shows genlock_holds + backward_steps + ts_present/ts_due/ts_head_skew, no SIGFPE, 30fps clean, drift-guard clean, #147 backward-step recovery). DO NOT MERGE (per dispatch) — supervisor reviews + merges.
- 📔 Playbook: genlock skill gained a "#147 backward wall-clock step recovery (SINK ts-align)" section (mechanism = head-future not last_wall_now, the backward_steps= audit signal, the 2500-char-window holds++ gotcha, the pinned tokens).

## #273 recording-verdict: trim foreign-run lead-in via --cam2-run-id optical pin (1.7.0-dev.123)
- BUG (proven on run 2706001): `frame_is_delivered_optical` ignored the pinned `--cam2-run-id`, so the strih recording's lead-in carrying the PREVIOUS run's residual cam2 paint (run_id 2606010, no cam1 burn) counted as current-run "delivery" → window anchored at frame 0 → 43 cam1-burn-absent lead-in frames charged BURN-UNREADABLE → false overall_pass=FALSE (chain actually zero-loss).
- FIX: thread `cam2_run_id: Option<u32>` through `frame_is_delivered_optical` (pinned ⇒ optical ONLY if `run_id == pin && !burns.contains` — the `!burns` is defense-in-depth, fails closed on a pin==burn misconfig) / `in_window_burn_frames` / `NodeSpec.cam2_run_id`. Covers fused AND --merge-partials paths (run_merge → build_and_print_verdict re-derives via `Args::cam2_pin()`, the `0⇒None` single source of truth). Leading trim = run-id-precise foreign-residue only; trailing = existing bounded #267 clamp; current-run-paint-absent-burn stays CHARGED (no masking, the #267 leading-edge guarantee). Empty/mis-pinned window ⇒ first_id None ⇒ is_contiguous() false ⇒ FAILS closed.
- RED 35f0c4f7f (`test:[red]`, verified FAIL with --features probe — foreign lead-in not trimmed) → GREEN 233c0d6d8 (`fix:[green] Closes #273`, 44/44 bin tests pass). Version bump a8710b693. Deep-review hardening 4c9ea041f (pin/burn-collision guard + test, boundary-assumption doc, Args::cam2_pin helper; 45/45 tests).
- REAL-DATA PROOF: re-merging the EXISTING run-2706001 partials (foreign 2606010 lead-in intact) with the fixed binary → EXIT=0, overall_pass true, burn_unreadable 43→0, cam1 contiguous 480..=9439 (0 missing), strih/stream zero-loss, V4L2 0 — matching verdict-2706001-CORRECTED.json's gate. Gives #109 phase-1 its clean 0-undecodable PASS.
- Tests: pinned_foreign_run_lead_in_is_trimmed_to_a_clean_pass, pinned_real_interior_loss_in_steady_span_still_fails (real-drop missing_ids=[52]), pinned_real_leading_current_run_loss_still_fails, frame_is_delivered_optical_honors_the_cam2_pin, frame_is_delivered_optical_pin_equal_to_a_burn_id_fails_closed. #267 clamp tests + merge-equivalence still green. PR #274. Reviews: /requesting-code-review no Critical; both Important + the Minor cleanup folded into 4c9ea041f.
- 📔 Playbook: recording-decode skill gained a "#273 GOTCHA — optical window must honor --cam2-run-id" section (the foreign-run lead-in residue bug, the run-id-precise-leading vs bounded-trailing trim + no-masking guarantees, empty-window fails-closed, the pin/burn defense-in-depth, and the local RED→GREEN re-merge recipe using the on-disk partials with the --features probe bypass).

## #275b cam1 async capture-burn → 60fps emit (worker, v1.7.0-dev.126, NOT merged) — 2026-06-27
- RESUME of the reopened #275: #275a (cheaper strih/stream burn) + #276 (multiview decouple) already merged+deployed; this is the last code piece (#275b — cam1 async burn so the cam→strih leg can be MEASURED at 60fps for #11).
- VALIDATED still real: issue #275 OPEN/reopened with the verified #275b design in the owner's comment; the live-rig A/B in the issue body (probe burn = 30.0 emitted / 62.5 captured vs prod 60.1/62.5) confirms the per-frame QR render on the emit loop caps cam1 at 30fps. Code premise confirmed against current src: `burn_qr_yuyv`/`render_payload_qr` ran synchronously in `main.rs process_frame` between the genlock gate and the NDI send.
- FIX (the design the #275 first pass DROPPED as risky, now resolved): dedicated `cam1-burn` thread + bounded FIFO ring (`src/probe/genlock.rs`: BurnFrameIdSource, BurnRing, burn_ring, run_burn_ring, BURN_RING_DEPTH=3). Capture thread stamps frame_id + emit-instant gen_ts + the gate-instant genlock boundary timecode AT THE GATE, copies the frame, submits; burn thread (owns the single NDI sender) renders the QR + sends with the CARRIED timecode (`ndi::boundary_timecode_100ns` + `NdiSender::send_frame_data_with_timecode`). The 3 feared hazards each resolved: per-frame gen_ts (carry the whole job, don't pre-render), genlock pacing jitter (timecode computed on capture thread at gate, not re-derived on send thread), 1:1 burn-id↔emit (BLOCKING sync_channel — back-pressures, never drops). Production path (probe off / env unset) = unchanged zero-copy send.

## #278 multiview ADAPTIVE budget-based decouple (worker, v1.7.0-dev.128, NOT merged) — 2026-06-27
- Supersedes #276's fixed render_divisor=2: rig-measured 4-LIVE-CAM multiview render ~18-23ms ALONE exceeds the 16.6ms 60fps budget → even every-other-frame the rendered frames overran → ~29% program renderSkip → program dropped to 43fps. #278 = ADAPTIVE budget skip so the PROGRAM is never starved.
- VALIDATED still real: current vendored obs-display.c carried the #276 fixed gate (`display->render_divisor>1 && (frame_counter++ % divisor)!=0`); the 4-live-cam 29%-renderSkip evidence is the live-rig finding on #278; graphics-thread premise confirmed (obs-video.c obs_graphics_thread_loop: frame_start → output_frames → render_displays → video_sleep).
- DESIGN (LIBOBS-ONLY, render_divisor>1 reused as the "throttleable monitoring display" marker — NO frontend change, fast obs.dll hot-swap suffices): obs-internal.h drops frame_counter, adds `uint64_t render_ewma_ns` (per-instance EWMA α=1/4, 0=cold) + obs_core_video `graphics_frame_start_ns`; obs-video.c publishes frame_start each tick; obs-display.c render_display() skips a monitoring display BEFORE render_display_begin() when `elapsed + ewma > interval - interval/10` (90% budget), ewma==0/no-timing → render to measure (never starve to 0), updates ewma `prev?(prev*3+dur)/4:dur` after a real render. Program/preview (divisor 0/1) NEVER throttled. Tradeoff: a multiview whose single render exceeds the whole budget freezes (accepted — program is the requirement; no over-budget probe so 0 renderSkip; close+reopen projector to re-warm).

## #280 cam1-burn buffer pool — recycle the async-burn frame copy (worker, v1.7.0-dev.129) — 2026-06-28
- Follow-up from the #275b/#279 review: the async cam1-burn copied each emitted frame with `data.to_vec()` (~4MB at 1080p YUYV) on the capture thread → a fresh heap alloc + free on EVERY emitted frame at up to 60fps. MEASUREMENT-ONLY path (probe + CAMERA_BOX_BURN_RUN_ID); production is the unchanged zero-copy send.
- VALIDATED still real: `src/main.rs:590` still `buf: data.to_vec()`; no `BufferPool` anywhere; no merged PR resolved it.
- DESIGN: `src/probe/genlock.rs` `BufferPool` (`Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>>` free list + `AtomicUsize` alloc counter), bounded `BURN_POOL_CAP = BURN_RING_DEPTH + 2 = 5`. Capture thread `take()`s (reuse, or alloc only when free list empty), `clear()`+`extend_from_slice` the mmap frame (reuses ~4MB capacity → no realloc), submits; burn thread `put()`s the buffer back after the NDI send. Pure memory opt — carries NO frame identity, so frame ORDER, the 1:1 burn_id↔emit mapping, and the carried gate-stamped timecode are all unchanged. Shutdown audit logs total allocations + idle count.
- RED a64bbe434 (`test:[red]`, BufferPool STUB: take always allocs, put no-op): `buffer_pool_recycles_a_returned_buffer_instead_of_reallocating` (free_len 0≠1) + `pooled_async_burn_recycles_buffers_and_preserves_1to1_ordering_under_backpressure` (500 allocations for 500 frames > 5) FAIL on the recycle assertion; ordering/timecode pass regardless. GREEN 0a34e1e6c (`fix:[green] Closes #280`: take pops free first, put pushes bounded, main.rs wired). Both green; existing #275b `async_burn_ring_preserves_1to1_mapping_in_order_under_backpressure` + #279 `submit_unblocks_on_shutdown_when_ring_full` + `burn_renders_qr_only_for_yuyv...` still pass.
- Tests file:line: `src/probe/genlock.rs` pool tests; impl `BufferPool`/`BURN_POOL_CAP` in same file; wiring `src/main.rs` (pool decl, burn-thread `put`, capture `take`+copy, shutdown log).

## #293 — strih OBS Multiview FROZEN (regression of #278): add anti-starvation floor (2026-06-28, SOLO PR)
- VALIDATED still real: read `vendor/obs-studio/libobs/obs-display.c` render_display() — the #278 skip `if (elapsed + ewma > budget) return;` had NO liveness floor; comment even admitted "may drop to a low fps / freeze under sustained over-budget load — fine". A 4-cam Multiview ewma (~18-23ms) alone > ~15ms budget every tick → skipped forever → frozen. RED C-harness proved the freeze (over-budget display never rendered over 240 ticks). Not solved by any later PR (git log obs-display.c: only #276→#278).
- DESIGN: extract the skip decision into a pure, OBS-dep-free header `vendor/obs-studio/libobs/obs-display-budget.h` — `obs_display_should_skip(divisor, ewma, elapsed, budget, consecutive_skips)` — so the EXACT production decision is unit-testable from a standalone `cc` harness under DEFAULT features (no probe build needed; probe-local builds are banned). Floor: `OBS_DISPLAY_MAX_CONSECUTIVE_SKIPS = 3u`; over-budget display skips only while `consecutive_skips < K`, the (K+1)-th over-budget tick is FORCED to render. New per-display `uint32_t render_consecutive_skips` (obs-internal.h), bumped on skip + reset to 0 after every real render in obs-display.c. Program (divisor<=1) and ewma==0 never skip — unchanged. Tradeoff (accepted in #293): a forced render every K+1 ticks costs ~1 program frame during sustained 4-cam overload — the user-chosen price of never-freeze (vs #278's 0% hit but frozen multiview); ~15fps multiview at K=3.
- RED d0d07a8c9 (`test:[red]`): `tests/obs_display_budget.rs` compiles+runs the REAL header (no-floor version) via `cc -std=c11`, replaying render_display()'s skip/render loop → "over-budget multiview NEVER rendered over 240 ticks (frozen)" → exit 1 → test FAILS. Locally verified RED.
- GREEN 9c9746db5 (`fix:[green] Closes #293`): floor added to the helper + obs-display.c wired (include, helper call, counter bump on skip, reset on render) + obs-internal.h field. Same test → PASS (over-budget renders within K+1; program never throttled; ewma==0 renders; under-budget renders every tick). Locally verified GREEN.
- Mirror guards updated (lock-step #269): `tests/genlock_preload.rs` (probe-gated, CI-only) display_render_adaptive_budget_gate_present + struct-fields + windows_genlock{,_fast}_workflow_gates now assert the helper call + `render_consecutive_skips` + the header floor instead of the old `elapsed + ewma > budget` string; `.github/workflows/windows-genlock{,-fast}.yml` pwsh source-anchor gates rewritten to the new strings (+ a new $bud read of obs-display-budget.h). `src/probe/genlock.rs` doc updated: the Rust `display_render_skip_budget` mirror is the BUDGET portion only; the floor lives in the C header. Verified all new mirror substrings present via a squish-simulation script.
- Local: version bump 41d310259 (dev.131→dev.132); Tier-0 default fmt/check/clippy -D warnings/test --no-run clean. The vendored C MSVC compile of obs-display-budget.h is the windows-genlock-fast.yml libobs build (fires on `vendor/obs-studio/**` push).
- RIG: NOT touched. NO OBS build/deploy to prod — vendored-OBS rollout is a coordinated supervisor step (batched with #152 title, #294 NDI-bg). The live 4-cam Multiview-unfreeze check is UNVERIFIED-on-rig (deferred to that rollout). PR dev→main, auto-merge.

## #292 — stream genlock latency capped at ~450ms (can't A/V-align to ~1s-late audio) (2026-06-28, SOLO PR)
- VALIDATED still real: read `vendor/obs-studio/libobs/obs-source.c genlock_source_drop_cap` — the per-source latency drop-cap budgeted `latency_frames` at the CANVAS output fps (`genlock_video_fps` = obs_get_video_info). But the ts-align deadline (`ready_async_frame`, `present_ts = wall_now - latency_ms`) holds every queued frame younger than latency_ms, so the FIFO fills at the SOURCE ARRIVAL rate. The stream box receives a 60fps NDI feed from strih into a 30fps canvas (the "60→30 strih→stream" topology, MEMORY final-mixed-60-30). At canvas fps, 1000ms budgeted 30 frames while the buffer parks ~60 → the overrun force-drain capped the delay ~half-way (~450ms, ≈27-29 frames near MAX_ASYNC_FRAMES=30 floor at 60fps). The #245 scaling + #257 UI int (max 2000) were present but used the WRONG fps. CI never caught it: the old `drop_cap_accommodates_deep_per_source_latency` budgeted at 30fps and always passed.
- ROOT CAUSE: drop-cap depth derived at canvas fps, not source arrival fps. new_max delivered: 2000ms (setter clamp unchanged) now actually delivered up to that at any source fps (was ~450ms). 2000ms@60fps = 120 frames + RESERVE 4 = cap 124 < abs-max 132 (GENLOCK_PRELOAD_MAX 128 unchanged — already sufficient; the binding constraint is depth+reserve, not abs-max).
- RED 3a5af16d8 (`test:[red]`): `tests/genlock_preload.rs` `drop_cap_delivers_high_latency_at_max_source_arrival` + `latency_depth_frames_budgets_at_source_arrival_rate` — extracted pure helper `genlock_latency_depth_frames(latency_ms, canvas_num, canvas_den)` with the buggy canvas-only body; cap 34 < 60 held @1000ms, helper returns 30 not 60. Proven RED locally via `cargo test --features probe --test genlock_preload  # airuleset:build-ok`.
- GREEN b22400d3f (`fix:[green] Closes #292`): helper budgets at max(canvas, arrival@GENLOCK_MAX_SOURCE_FPS=60); C `genlock_source_drop_cap` mirrors it; new `#define GENLOCK_MAX_SOURCE_FPS 60` (C) + `pub const GENLOCK_MAX_SOURCE_FPS: u32 = 60` (Rust mirror). C divide `(latency_ms*60+500)/1000` exactly mirrors `ms_to_frames(ms,60,1)`. 97 genlock tests GREEN locally.
- Mirror guard (lock-step #269): new `drop_cap_budgets_at_source_arrival_fps_in_vendored_source` pins `#define GENLOCK_MAX_SOURCE_FPS 60` + `source->genlock_latency_ms * GENLOCK_MAX_SOURCE_FPS` in obs-source.c so a subtree revert can't silently re-cap latency. YAML gates unchanged (GENLOCK_PRELOAD_MAX still 128, genlock_source_drop_cap still present).
- Local: version bump 3797ea9a1 (dev.133→dev.134); Tier-0 default fmt/check/clippy -D warnings/test --no-run clean; clippy --features probe clean.
- RIG: NOT touched. NO OBS build/deploy — vendored-OBS rollout is a coordinated supervisor step (batched with #152 title, #293 multiview, upcoming #294/#297). Live "set 1000ms on stream, measure A/V align" is UNVERIFIED-on-rig (deferred to that rollout); the unit test (drop-cap delivers the requested latency up to 2000ms at 60fps, no 450 clamp) is the code-level proof. PR dev→main, auto-merge.

The dual-QR `vernier_ids` ALREADY emits a distinct id per refresh — the bug was the RATE, never the id logic.

## #313 — REGRESSION (#152): NewlevelBuildDate() crashed OBS at startup (std::_Xran) (2026-06-28, SOLO PR)
- ROOT CAUSE: #152's title-bar date helper `NewlevelBuildDate()` (`vendor/obs-studio/frontend/widgets/OBSBasic.cpp`) parsed the compiler `__DATE__` with NO size guard — `d.substr(7, 4)` throws `std::out_of_range` (MSVC `std::_Xran` "invalid string position") when the compile-date string is not the expected 11-char "Mmm DD YYYY". The throw propagated `NewlevelBuildDate()`→`UpdateTitleBar()`→`OBSBasic` ctor→`OBSApp::OBSInit` → OBS aborted at startup on EVERY launch (confirmed on the live strih box). A title-bar helper must NEVER crash OBS.
- FIX: extracted the parse into the pure, OBS/Qt-free `newlevel_iso_date(const std::string&)` in a new header `vendor/obs-studio/frontend/widgets/NewlevelBuildDate.hpp`, guarded `if (compileDate.size() < 11) return "unknown";` BEFORE any `d[..]`/`substr`. `NewlevelBuildDate()` keeps the pinned anchor `const std::string d = __DATE__;` then `return newlevel_iso_date(d);` — so BOTH `windows-genlock{,-fast}.yml` source gates + `tests/obs_titlebar_newlevel.rs` stay GREEN with NO lock-step change (gated_line_kept=yes).
- RED ee58a5e2c (`test:[red]`): `tests/obs_titlebar_newlevel_parse.rs::newlevel_iso_date_never_throws_on_short_or_empty_date` — compiles+runs the real header in a tiny C++ harness (`c++ -std=c++17`, default features, no probe). RED proven locally (harness threw `substr __pos(7) > size(6)` → abort → FAIL).
- GREEN e2585c0b7 (`fix:[green] Closes #313`): size guard added → short/empty→"unknown", "Jun 28 2026"→"2026-06-28", space-padded "Jan  1 2020"→"2020-01-01" → test PASSES.
- TESTABILITY PATTERN: the OBS FRONTEND compiles ONLY on the 150-min `windows-genlock.yml` (invisible to PR CI), so frontend C++ bugs escape normal CI. Extending the #293 pure-header harness pattern to C++ (`obs_display_budget.rs` is the `cc -std=c11` twin) lets a default-features Rust test exercise the real frontend code off-rig. Captured in the genlock skill.
- Local: version bump 5df15539e (dev.137→dev.138); Tier-0 default fmt/check/clippy -D warnings/test --no-run clean; both `obs_titlebar_newlevel.rs` anchor tests + the new parse test GREEN (built-binary-direct, no probe, no bypass).
- RIG: NOT touched. NO OBS build/deploy, NO win-* MCP — the supervisor rebuilds the genlock OBS from this merged fix and redeploys+retests (strih already rolled back to the known-good build). PR #314 dev→main, auto-merge.

## #297 — NDI sender re-announce for reliable OBS discovery (2026-06-28/29)
- Problem: OBS/DistroAV mDNS NDI discovery is flaky; the camera-box NDI sender is created ONCE at startup (`NDIlib_send_create`) and never re-registers, so a box whose network came up after start (boot race / late DHCP) or whose link flapped announces on a dead/old network and is never rediscovered (observed live: finder returned only {CAM2} while CAM1/3/4 were up).
- Fix (option 3, sender-side): re-register the sender (re-runs the mDNS announce on the CURRENT network) when the host's usable LAN address set changes OR recovers from an outage.
  - `src/reannounce.rs` (pure, cross-platform, unit-tested): `NetworkSignature` (order/dup-independent set), `should_reannounce(announced, current, saw_down_since_announce)` (re-announce iff current non-empty AND (changed OR recovered-from-down)), `is_discoverable_interface()` (LAN-NIC denylist: drops lo/docker/virbr/veth/tailscale/tun/wg/…), `REANNOUNCE_POLL_INTERVAL=2s`.
  - `src/ndi.rs` (Linux IO): `current_network_signature()` via `getifaddrs` (up/non-loopback/IPv4, LAN-NIC only, `Ipv4Addr` render); `NdiSender::maybe_reannounce()` (throttled) + `reannounce_now()` (create-new-then-destroy-old → always a valid sender; `saw_down_since_announce` tracked).
  - `src/main.rs`: capture loop calls `maybe_reannounce()` each successful-capture tick (non-probe `sender`; probe `capture_sender.as_mut()` — burn mode skips, sender on burn thread).
- TDD: RED 2cb323187 (`test:[red]`) stub `should_reannounce→false` (the "never re-announces" bug) → 2 network-change cases FAIL; GREEN c7d7c305a (`fix:[green] Closes #297`); review fixes 7f5d66afb (LAN filter + down→up) + 04b0a7c3d (Ipv4Addr + docs). `tests/ndi_reannounce.rs` 12 tests GREEN (built-binary-direct, default features, no probe).
- Reviews: `/code-review` (high) + `superpowers:requesting-code-review` — no Critical/blocking; all actionable minors fixed in-PR. Deep review's Important#1 = a rig-verification gate (boxes must have been in boot-race/flap state, NOT stable-lost-announce) → UNVERIFIED-on-rig (supervisor verifies discovery).
- SCOPE: sender-side re-announce handles boot-race/late-DHCP/link-flap. The PERSISTENT mDNS/multicast flakiness for STABLE boxes (the central NDI Discovery Server option 1, or LAN multicast fix option 2) is a separate fleet-infra decision raised to the user (architecture question).
- PR #315 dev→main, merged d15b540c0 (auto-merge default; no manual marker). Version 1.7.0-dev.139.
- RIG: NOT touched (no deploy to cam boxes, no win-*/cam MCP) — supervisor deploys + verifies discovery on the rig.

## #297 (re-opened) — NDI sender re-announce was an infinite failing loop (2026-06-29)
- Symptom (live, CAM2 .62 reboot, dev.139): `reannounce_now` looped every 2s — `network changed [] -> [ips]` → `NDIlib_send_create returned null; kept existing sender` forever; boot-raced box never rediscovered.
- Root cause: the prior cycle's `create-new-then-destroy-old` is SELF-DEFEATING — the NDI SDK refuses a 2nd sender with a name already live in the process, so a same-name `send_create` while the old handle lives ALWAYS returns null → `bail!` → `announced_sig` never advances from the boot `[]` baseline → `should_reannounce([], [ips], saw_down)` stays true → re-fires every poll.
- Fix (`src/ndi.rs` `reannounce_now`): DESTROY old FIRST (guard null handle like `Drop`), THEN `send_create` the fresh same-name sender. Advance the trigger ONLY on create success; on the rare null-create-after-destroy leave `self.sender` null + state unchanged (retry next poll). Emit path `send_frame_data_with_timecode` GUARDS a null sender (drop frame, never `send_send_video_v2(NULL)`=UB). #3 (seed `announced_sig` with live net at create) was already in dev.139 — the `[]` baseline came from a genuine boot race, not a missing init.
- Refactor: trigger state+transitions extracted to pure, unit-tested `crate::reannounce::ReannounceState` (`new`/`should_reannounce`/`mark_down`/`record_reannounce_attempt`).
- TDD: RED d3cf41fb6 (`test:[red]`) 4 pure tests reference not-yet-existent `ReannounceState` → fail to compile; GREEN 5d7f9a0b0 (`fix:[green] Closes #297`) → 4/4 pass + 12/12 existing integration. Review fixes: f243deb07 (null-guard on destroy retry path, `/code-review`), 12f20530f (drop dead `Default` derive, deep review).
- Reviews: `/code-review` (xhigh) found+fixed the destroy-null-on-retry bug; `superpowers:requesting-code-review` (deep) — no Critical/Important correctness; Important#1 = pure tests lock convergence not the literal FFI ordering → filed #317 (FFI test-seam, fails bundling gate: needs a mockable NdiLib function table separate from `libloading::Library`).
- PR #316 dev→main. Version 1.7.0-dev.140. RIG: NOT touched — supervisor deploys to fleet + reboot-verifies CAM2.
- #297 NDI sender re-announce infinite loop fixed (destroy-before-create + init announced_sig) — PR #316 merged febc4a5, dev.140 deployed CAM1/2/4, CAM2 reboot-verified: 0 null-creates, converges, strih reconnected :5961. (#297-A boot test)

## #317 — lock NDI re-announce FFI ordering (destroy-before-create) via a mockable function table (2026-06-29)
- Gap (from #316 deep review): the pure `crate::reannounce` tests lock the convergence/retry CONTRACT but NEVER call the FFI, so a revert of `reannounce_now` to create-first would keep all 4 pure tests green while reintroducing the #297 infinite loop. The literal `send_destroy`-before-`send_create` order was enforced only by doc-comment + review.
- Seam (`src/ndi.rs`): added private `trait NdiSendOps { send_create; send_destroy }` separating the two FFI calls from the `libloading::Library`. `NdiLib` impls it as a thin `#[inline]` pass-through to the loaded raw fn pointers (live path byte-identical). Extracted the destroy-first dance into `fn reannounce_dance<O: NdiSendOps>(ops, &mut sender, settings, &mut trigger, current) -> bool`; `reannounce_now` now delegates to it. No behavior change.
- Test (`mod ffi_seam_tests`): `FakeNdi` records `[Op::Destroy(ptr)|Op::Create]` order and models the SDK one-live-sender-per-name rule (create while a same-name handle is live → null = the create-first trap). 3 tests: `reannounce_destroys_old_before_creating_new_and_advances_trigger` (a+c), `null_create_after_destroy_leaves_sender_null_and_trigger_unchanged` (b), `retry_after_failed_create_then_succeeds` (null-slot retry skips destroy, the Drop-null guard).
- RED-on-revert proof: working-tree revert to create-first → recorded order `[Create, Destroy(4096)]`, tests (a) and (b) FAIL ("MUST destroy the old sender BEFORE creating"); restored → 3/3 GREEN + 214/214 lib + 4/4 pure reannounce.
- Tier-0 local: fmt/check/clippy(-D warnings) clean, default features only. Verified ENTIRELY in CI — NO rig/ssh/OBS. Version 1.7.0-dev.141.
- #317 NDI re-announce FFI ordering locked via NdiSendOps mockable test seam — PR #318 merged 600da602, main CI green. Test-seam only (no behavior change), fleet stays dev.140.

## #309 — rig-test #291 no-display drop-in leaked into sibling e2e harness restores (1.7.0-dev.142)
- Bug (still valid, verified by reading current code): `rig-mode.sh test` installs a TRANSIENT no-display systemd drop-in (`/run/.../zz-rig-test-no-display.conf`, #291) to free /dev/fb0. `recording-e2e.sh` cleanup (`systemctl restart camera-box`, line ~234) and `loopback-e2e.sh` remote cleanup (`systemctl start camera-box`, line ~102) restored camera-box WITHOUT removing it → a leftover from a prior `rig-mode.sh test` brings camera-box back in no-display mode (dark interkom return monitor) while the operator believes broadcast is restored (#246-class silent leak). recording-e2e itself uses a full `systemctl stop` (not the drop-in), so the fix is purely defensive against an operator leftover.
- Fix (DRY single-source): new `scripts/lib/rig-test-dropin.sh` owns the `RIG_TEST_DROPIN` path constant + a PURE `rig_test_dropin_clear_cmds [DROPIN]` builder (emits `rm -f` drop-in + `rmdir` + `daemon-reload`, all `|| true` idempotent). rig-mode.sh sources it (painter_stop_remote now uses the builder; literal path removed from all 3 of its sites); recording-e2e.sh injects `$(rig_test_dropin_clear_cmds)` into the cam2 ssh restore before the restart; loopback-e2e.sh carries it to the remote shell via `build_remote_env`'s `RIG_TEST_DROPIN_CLEAR=%q` then `eval "${RIG_TEST_DROPIN_CLEAR:-}"` in the remote cleanup before the start. Path now lives in exactly ONE place.
- RED→GREEN: `tests/harness_rig_test_dropin_cleanup.rs` (6 tests). RED commit 5dcb0b96b — 6/6 FAIL (no lib, no harness wiring). GREEN commit 5f4ba2197 — 6/6 PASS. Headline behavioral test `shared_clear_removes_the_dropin` runs the builder's emitted bash against a fake drop-in and asserts the file is gone (proven without ssh). No regression: rig_mode 14/14, harness_recording_e2e_paths 37/37, harness_remote_env_quoting 1/1 (the #39 injection-safety still holds with the added RIG_TEST_DROPIN_CLEAR field).
- Gotcha for next time: ANY new path that restarts cam2's camera-box must source the helper + clear the drop-in before restart/start (added to `.claude/skills/e2e` SKILL.md). Tier-0 local fmt/check/clippy(-D warnings) clean, default features. Verified ENTIRELY in CI — NO rig/ssh/OBS.

## #328 — obs_phase2 prod-scene/teardown HANG (no overall timeout) → blocked #312 run + held cam1 /dev/video0
- Version 1.7.0-dev.149 (commit 6d2919fa8). Solo PR; bug fix, regression-test-first.
- ROOT CAUSE: `obs_phase2.py` `_rpc`'s read loop drains op-5 EVENTS until the matching op-7 response; while OBS renegotiates an NDI source it floods events, so every `recv()` keeps succeeding within the 10s socket timeout yet the response never comes → the loop spins forever (the RUN_ID 312001 ~28-min prod-scene, then teardown, hang). NOT a sick WS — purely no overall wall-clock deadline. The hung teardown then never reached cam1's device restore, so the #174 burn binary kept holding /dev/video0 → prod camera-box crash-looped (#281 class).
- FIX 1 (commit 8da0a978e): added `OBS_OP_TIMEOUT_S` (default 60s, env-overridable) + pure `_rpc_timed_out(elapsed,timeout)` helper; `_rpc` raises `TimeoutError` past the deadline (fail loud → non-zero exit for prod-scene/setup/switch, caught+warned inside teardown's best-effort guard). `ignore_err` suppresses a normal failed RPC but NEVER the timeout. Also catches `WebSocketTimeoutException` to re-check the deadline on socket silence.
- FIX 2 (commit 8da0a978e): `recording-e2e.sh` cleanup() now frees the cam1/cam2 capture devices FIRST (pkill -9 -f the burn binary, rm, restart camera-box) before/independent of the OBS restore, and wraps every cam ssh + every obs_phase2/obs_burn_filter call in `timeout` (`CLEANUP_SSH_TIMEOUT`=30s, `OBS_CLEANUP_TIMEOUT`=90s, env-overridable). A stuck OBS op can no longer strand /dev/video0.
- Tests: RED ec139ed08 → GREEN 8da0a978e. `tests/python/test_obs_phase2_timeout.py` (pure deadline helper + named/env-overridable constant, 6 cases). `tests/harness_recording_e2e_cleanup_resilient.rs` (cam-device-free BEFORE obs teardown; every OBS call timeout-bounded; pkill -9 force-kill). Python 60/60 local; Rust CI-run.
- Commit hygiene: NO `fix:/Closes/Fixes/Resolves #312` anywhere (would auto-close #312); only `Part of #312`. `Closes #328` on the fix commit + PR body.

## 2026-06-29 — #334 (solo clean-code PR, NO rig)
- Version 1.7.0-dev.151 (commit cc0b429ad). Solo PR; pure Python fix to `scripts/obs_burn_filter.py` + a Python unit test. NO C++/rig (supervisor does rig verification — #281 discipline).
- #334 (Closes): the strih(911002)+stream(911004) measurement burns were ABSENT from the all-cambox recording because the `DistroAV QR Burn (latency probe)` EFFECT filter on the program input was `filterEnabled=False` (confirmed live by the supervisor: `GetSourceFilterList` on strih `NDI cam5` → ENABLED=False). A disabled effect filter's `video_render` is never invoked, so the burn never renders even though `genlock_burn=true` and the C++ setter fires (OBS log shows `genlock: measurement burn ON`). Pure obs_burn_filter.py bug, NOT a C++/genlock bug.
- Two defects fixed: (1) `cmd_check` computed `burn_on = (genlock_burn is True) and present` — no enabled check, so a present-but-DISABLED filter reported a FALSE-POSITIVE `burn_on=True`, letting `recording-e2e.sh [4b/8]` pre-record gate pass with no burn (#195/#257 the gate was meant to catch this). New pure `compute_burn_on(genlock_burn, present, enabled)` requires all three; `cmd_check` now also prints `filter_enabled=<bool>`. (2) `_ensure_filter` early-returned when the filter existed, so `cmd_add` never re-enabled a present-but-disabled filter — now it unconditionally (re-)enables (idempotent). New `_filter_enabled(ws, input)` helper reads `filterEnabled` from `GetSourceFilterList`.
- RED 820a363bd → GREEN 2f588d343. Tests (`tests/python/test_obs_burn_filter.py`, drives a fake `_rpc`, no live OBS): `test_compute_burn_on_false_when_filter_disabled` (the regression), `test_cmd_check_burn_on_false_when_present_but_disabled` (behavioral false-positive RED), `test_cmd_add_reenables_present_but_disabled_filter`. Full python suite 66 passed, ruff clean.
- RIG VERIFICATION PENDING — supervisor must (a) run `obs_burn_filter.py add` on strih `NDI cam5` + stream `NDI 2ME PGM` via the FIXED script and confirm `check` now reports `burn_on` only when `filter_enabled=True`, and (b) re-run an ALL_CAMBOX `recording-e2e.sh` and confirm strih(911002)+stream(911004) burns are PRESENT and per-hop latency is measured.

## 2026-06-30 — #360 verdict cam2-tick gating + strih free-running step (worker, v1.7.0-dev.162)

- **Root cause (2 cooperating)**: (1) `in_window_burn_frames` windowed strih/stream (PerRenderTick) burn contiguity to OPTICAL-only frames → at high latency the cam2 dual-QR was ~87% (354003) / ~91% (354001) undecodable, excising burn-present delivered frames; (2) `node_render_step` assumed strih steps by clean `round(60/30)=2`, but the strih burn is a FREE-RUNNING render-tick stepping irregularly 0–10 (mean ~4) → the step-2 excess-gap math charged ~17300 phantom REAL DROPs.
- **Fix** (`src/bin/recording-verdict.rs`): membership now `is_optical(f) || has_node_burn(f)` for ALL rates (cam2 optical no longer gates the digital burn-chain; extends cam1 #204); `node_render_step` → 1 for all nodes (strih gap-ignore). `burn_contiguity_in_window_with_step` step≥2 capability + #11 tests retained but unused by any node.
- **RED** `7d391df8a` (`test:[red]` — `node_verdict_strih_zero_loss_when_cam2_optical_mostly_undecodable_360`, RED proved: expected_count=11 optical-only + 130 phantom). **GREEN** `fd9ab4e0b` (`fix:[green]`). Updated 2 obsolete tests that encoded old behavior; added `strih_burn_on_a_non_optical_frame_inside_span_is_included_360` + `node_render_step_is_gap_ignore_for_all_nodes_360`.
- **Validated** (sanctioned probe re-merge, purged after): 354003 strih 17300→0, stream 0; 354001 (#356's run) strih 17829→0. Full suite: bin 49/49, lib 607/607.
- **PROOF strih gaps are jitter not loss**: every strih gap>8 on 354003 coincided with a CLEAN stream-burn step (stream burn never gapped = zero stream-output loss).
- **#356 kept OPEN** (not closed): the #360 fix removes the DOMINANT over-count (strih 17829/20080 on 354001) but the residual cam1 over-count (2251/2297, softened cam1 burn in the strih recording at high latency, 0% present downstream — a cam1-burn READABILITY limit, distinct from the cam2-tick bug) persists → #356 now tracks that.
- **CLI surface unchanged** — re-merge with the SAME flags: `recording-verdict --merge-partials strih=… --merge-partials stream=… --min-secs 300 --capture-fps 60 --strih-emit-fps 60 --stream-capture-fps 30 --cam2-run-id <run> --burn-cam1-run-id 911001 --burn-strih-run-id 911002 --burn-stream-run-id 911004 --json out.json`.
- No deploy (probe binaries are CI-built artifacts; no rig touched — pure code PR).

## 2026-06-30 — #363 restore the HARD optical dual-QR gate in recording-verdict (clean-code PR, NO rig, v1.7.0-dev.166)

- **Why**: #360 (PR #361) weakened `recording-verdict` so the in-window membership became `is_optical(f) || has_node_burn(f)` — a frame with ONLY a software-injected digital burn (no cam2 OPTICAL read) counted as delivered, so an 87%-optically-undecodable run PASSED on the digital burns alone (the fraud the user flagged). The cam2 OPTICAL dual-QR is the ONLY proof of the real camera-captured pixel path; the burns are injected AFTER capture (node→node digital delivery only).
- **Fix** (`src/bin/recording-verdict.rs`): membership reverted to OPTICAL-only for strih/stream (PerRenderTick): `is_optical(f) || (matches!(rate, PerEmittedFrame) && has_node_burn(f))`. cam1 (PerEmittedFrame) KEEPS its burn in-window (the #204 per-emit anti-phantom fix — its burn is genuine per-emit delivery proof), so removing the fallback for it would re-introduce phantom drops; the task's "NOT a phantom chain drop" is honoured.
- **New distinct hard-fail**: `optical_undecodable` — in-span frames whose cam2 optical QR did not decode. New helpers `optical_span()` + `optical_undecodable_in_span()` (single source of the optical boundary). `NodeVerdict.optical_undecodable` field; `is_zero() = contiguity.is_contiguous() && optical_undecodable == 0`. Surfaced in `print_node_verdict` + `node_verdict_json`. An undecodable in-span frame is NEITHER a phantom chain drop (pre-#360) NOR a pass (#360) — it is its own OPTICAL-UNDECODABLE category. No flag/env/threshold/tolerance — the gate is hard and stays hard.
- **TDD** (probe-gated, CI-only — `recording-verdict.rs` is `required-features=["probe"]`, not buildable/testable locally per CLAUDE.md): RED `07673a2ad` → GREEN `eda38cad1`. New test `node_verdict_optical_undecodable_is_a_hard_fail_363` (minimal 3-frame lock). Inverted the two #360 tests: `node_verdict_strih_zero_loss_when_cam2_optical_mostly_undecodable_360` → `node_verdict_fails_when_cam2_optical_mostly_undecodable_363` (87% case now FAILS, optical_undecodable=60, expected_count=11, real_drops=0); `strih_burn_on_a_non_optical_frame_inside_span_is_included_360` → `..._excluded_and_undecodable_363` (non-optical strih frame now excluded + counted undecodable). Updated the #204 test `cam1_burn_on_an_optical_blurred_frame_is_not_a_phantom_drop`: the run now FAILS on optical-undecodable but cam1 still reports NO phantom drop (the #204 anti-phantom contract — locked at the `burn_contiguity_in_window` level by the unchanged `tests/recording_verdict.rs` integration test).
- **Scope**: ONLY the verdict gate logic + its unit tests. Did NOT touch optical-decode robustness (QR size/painter/moiré/focus/exposure) — that is rig work the supervisor drives separately. Issue #363 stays OPEN (PR references it, no Closes) for the rig optical-robustness work.
- No deploy (probe binaries are CI-built artifacts; camera-box has no web dashboard).

## 2026-06-30 — #363 optical-robustness half: decode_qr_luma_all merges plain ∪ Otsu (clean-code PR, NO rig, v1.7.0-dev.167)

- **Why**: after #372 restored the HARD optical gate (`optical_undecodable == 0`), the stream recording still showed ~87% optical-undecodable — but the cam2 optical dual-QR pixels were PRESENT and readable. A DECODER gating bug, not the camera.
- **Root cause** (`src/probe/qr.rs::decode_qr_luma_all`): the Otsu-binarized rqrr retry ran ONLY when the plain pass found NOTHING (`if !first.is_empty() { return first }`). On the stream recording the plain pass reads the crisp DIGITAL BURNS (non-empty) but MISSES the SOFT optical dual-QR (filmed off a monitor: low-contrast + moiré + colour-cast) → the Otsu retry was skipped → the present optical QR was never recovered. PROOF: on the real failing frames f-5.png/f-150.png (run 354003) rqrr returns the optical dual-QR when `binarize_otsu`-ed but NONE on the plain pass.
- **Fix**: ALWAYS run both rqrr passes (plain AND Otsu-binarized) and `merge_payloads` (de-dup by `(run_id,frame_id)`). Result is a SUPERSET of the plain pass. Fixes `decode_qr_luma_all_robust` + `decode_qr_luma_all_fast_then_robust` for free. Cost: +1 cheap full-frame Otsu rqrr pass per OFFLINE-decoded frame; the ~10× tiles stay conditional. `decode_qr_luma_all_tile` unchanged (tile gating is about small node burns, not the soft optical).
- **TDD** (probe-gated, CI-only): version bump `1752e0b26` → RED `27ad90715` → GREEN `77508e3cc`. New test `optical_soft_dual_qr_recovered_on_real_stream_frames` + fixtures `tests/fixtures/optical-soft-f5.png` / `optical-soft-f150.png` (real run-354003 frames): plain pass returns NO run_id 354003; `decode_qr_luma_all` returns BOTH optical halves; the recording per-frame path surfaces it too. Verified locally RED (`decode_qr_luma_all must recover BOTH optical dual-QR halves … got optical frame_ids []`) → GREEN.
- **Test adaptations** (the fix strengthened the full-frame pass — the Otsu union now reads, full-frame, the synthetic 260px/blur-2.8 burn AND 3 of 5 real burn-unreadable fixtures, flipping them to the FAST path — a real improvement): the synthetic "full-frame MISSES it" premise no longer reproduces. `robust_recovers_a_softened_burn_the_full_frame_pass_misses` → `full_frame_otsu_union_recovers_a_softened_burn_bare_rqrr_misses` (now locks the #363 improvement: bare single rqrr misses → Otsu union recovers, same synthetic input). `robust_fallback_taken_when_plain_misses_an_expected_burn` → `fast_then_robust_falls_back_to_robust_on_a_real_burn_unreadable_frame` (the genuine full-frame-miss/tiles-recover gap is detector-coverage on real 4K pixels → repointed to real fixture cam1-frame-1148, burn 911001.1727). `tests/burn_fixture_decode.rs` still green (4/4).
- **Local verify** (sanctioned probe RED→GREEN exception): `cargo build/test --features probe` with `AIRULESET_ALLOW_LOCAL_BUILD=1 # airuleset:build-ok`; qr unit suite 25/25, burn_fixture 4/4, recording_decode 3/3, recording_verdict 12/12, clippy --features probe clean. `cargo clean` after to bound the shared dev1 target/ (#185).
- **Scope**: ONLY `src/probe/qr.rs` (+ fixtures, skill). Did NOT touch the verdict gate logic (recording-verdict.rs is_zero/membership) — that is #372, already merged.
- Closes #363. No deploy (probe binaries are CI-built artifacts; camera-box has no web dashboard).

## 2026-06-30 — #364 E2E STRICT GATE: per-camera COLOUR correctness (clean-code PR, supervisor drives rig proof)

- **Issue**: #364 — the zero-loss verdict proves frame DELIVERY only; colour is never checked, so a grayscale / hue-shifted / cast camera delivers every frame and PASSES. Built the HARD gate that FAILS on it (mirrors the #363 optical read).
- **Version**: 1.7.0-dev.167 → 1.7.0-dev.168 (Cargo.toml, first commit `7651d470f`).
- **Pure module** `src/colour_verify.rs` (Tier-0, default features — the regression lock): iterates the SAME `colour_scale::colour_scale_patches()` / `PATCH_COLOURS` the #367 painter writes (ONE source of truth). Sampler `sample_patch_means` takes each patch's mean over pixels NOT inside the burn-exclusion rects (dodge). `classify_patch` decides per patch: chromatic → Grayscale (chroma < `GRAYSCALE_CHROMA_MIN` 40) / HueShift (hue err > `HUE_TOL_DEG` 30) / OutOfTolerance (sRGB dist > `SRGB_DIST_TOL` 96); neutral → NeutralTint (chroma > `NEUTRAL_CHROMA_MAX` 48) / OutOfTolerance. Burn-covered patch = `Unsamplable` → SKIPPED not charged (a global defect still fails on the visible patches); fail-closed only when nothing checkable. `summarize_node_colour` reduces per-frame verdicts to a node verdict via STRICT-majority vote.
- **RED→GREEN** (`bug`-labeled → regression-test-first): `test:[red]` `0763258ff` (stubbed `classify_patch` → grayscale/hue/tint tests FAIL — observed) → `fix:[green]` `0eb8b6a9e` (real decision → all GREEN). Named lock: `colour_gate_passes_on_correct_fails_on_grayscale_and_hue_shift`. Plus `tolerance_boundaries_are_exact` (each threshold pinned at its exact integer-RGB-reachable boundary — mutation-hardening) and the strict-majority tie case. 16 default-feature tests, all green locally (`# airuleset:build-ok`).
- **Probe glue** `src/probe/colour_sample.rs` (CI-only): `node_burn_exclusions` derives cam1 (`qr::cam1_burn_origin`, 320px) + strih/stream corner burns (`burn_geom::corner_placement`, 0.28·h ≈302px on 1080, margin 40/1080·h) — all bottom-anchored leaving the band's bottom strip clear so every patch stays samplable; `colour_verdict_from_rgb_image` (RgbImage→verdict); `extract_recording_colour_summary` (ffmpeg input-seek N frames, bounded). Thin probe test (no ffmpeg).
- **Gate wiring** `src/bin/recording-verdict.rs`: `NodeVerdict.colour_fail` gates `is_zero()` (`== 0`, alongside contiguity + `optical_undecodable`); printed + JSON. `--colour-gate`/`--colour-samples` flags. `build_node_colour_fail` (ffmpeg I/O glue) populates it on the fused/on-host path; merge mode (no rec_path) errors loudly (never silently skips a requested gate). Default OFF ⇒ delivery-only runs unchanged. Lock: `node_verdict_colour_fail_is_a_hard_fail_364`.
- **CI** (`ci.yml`): `colour_sample.rs` added to the coverage + mutants exclusions (I/O glue, like decode_for/run_merge); `build_node_colour_fail` excluded from mutants. The JUDGEMENT (`colour_verify.rs`) stays covered + mutated.
- **Follow-up filed** #377: carry the per-camera colour verdict through the #208 extract+merge path (store the per-box colour aggregate in `RecordingPartial`, read in merge) so `--colour-gate` works in the cross-box production flow too.
- **Scope**: clean code + synthetic-fixture tests only. Did NOT touch the rig — the SUPERVISOR drives the rig colour-capture proof + adds the real recorded fixture (incl. a bad-colour variant) that locks the gate end-to-end, and confirms the burn-dodge geometry on the rig. Closes #364 (gate mechanism + pure logic). No web dashboard (probe binaries are CI-built artifacts).

### 2026-06-30 (cont.) — #364 colour scale → vertical column in the central dual-QR gap (geometry move)

- **Why**: rig proof showed the BOTTOM-band colour scale (y=960..1080) is CROPPED off the bottom by the camera's framing of the cam2 monitor — it never reached the recording, so the gate had nothing to sample. The cam2 PAINTED source (fb0) is clean; only the bottom band was out of frame. The two dual-QR halves ARE captured (they decode), so the central gap between them is reliably in frame.
- **Version**: 1.7.0-dev.168 → 1.7.0-dev.169 (Cargo.toml, commit `06a7577dd`). Same `dev` / PR #378 (Closes #364).
- **Change** (geometry ONLY — did NOT touch `classify_patch` / tolerances; the supervisor calibrates on live rig data): `colour_scale::colour_scale_patches` now takes `(canvas_w, canvas_h, qr_size, top_margin)` and derives the central gap with the SAME formula `blit_qr_bgra`/`render_qr_dual_bgra` use (half = canvas_w/2; each QR centered in its half via `(band_w−qw)/2`; top-anchored at `top_margin` over `qh = qr_size.min(canvas_h)`). New `dual_qr_gap()` helper returns the gap `[left_qr_end, right_qr_start) × [qr_top, qr_bottom)` or `None` when degenerate. The 13 `PATCH_COLOURS` (unchanged — gate contract) stack as a VERTICAL column inside the gap (10px inset each side → x∈[840,1080), y∈[24,724) at default 1920×1080/qr 700/tm 24), last patch absorbs the integer remainder. Removed the old `BAND_H` bottom band.
- **Single source of truth**: both call sites derive the SAME rects — painter `qr::blit_colour_scale_bgra(canvas, w, h, qr_size, top_margin)` (painter.rs passes `params.qr_size` + `qr::TOP_MARGIN_PX`); gate sampler `colour_verify::sample_patch_means`/`verify_rgb_frame` + `colour_sample::{colour_verdict_from_rgb_image,extract_recording_colour_summary}` thread `qr_size`/`top_margin`; `recording-verdict.rs build_node_colour_fail` passes `colour_scale::{DEFAULT_QR_SIZE,TOP_MARGIN_PX}`. `qr::TOP_MARGIN_PX` is now defined as `colour_scale::TOP_MARGIN_PX` (one literal). The column ends at the QR bottom (~724) above all three bottom-anchored burns, so burn-exclusion rects became belt-and-braces (no patch loses pixels).
- **RED→GREEN** (Tier-0, observed `cargo test --lib colour # airuleset:build-ok`): `test:[red]` `1bd8aa1bd` — new central-gap tests with the legacy bottom-band stub body ⇒ 5 FAIL (`every_patch_is_inside_the_central_gap_and_within_canvas`, `no_patch_intersects_a_qr_half_or_any_burn`, `patches_tile_the_column_top_to_bottom_contiguously`, `degenerate_or_too_small_gap_yields_no_patches`, + `colour_verify::sampler_dodges_a_burn_column...`). `fix:[green]` `22ab6e0ec` — real gap-derived column ⇒ all 23 colour tests GREEN. Tests mirror the QR-half + cam1-center-burn + corner-burn rects as consts (like the prior QR mirror) and assert no patch intersects any of them.
- **Scope**: clean code + Tier-0 tests only. Did NOT touch the rig/OBS/recordings — the SUPERVISOR owns the live rig re-capture, the gate tolerance calibration on real data, the real fixture, and the merge. No web dashboard (probe binaries are CI-built artifacts).
